5:T586,I’m working with two agents in Eliza:

-   **Blockchain Agent** – handles blockchain transactions.
-   **Support Agent** – a RAG-based agent for answering support queries.

### 🧠 Use Case

A user starts a conversation with the blockchain agent. At some point, they ask a question the blockchain agent can’t answer, but the support agent can.

I want the blockchain agent to **pass the question internally to the support agent**, get the response, and send it back to the user — seamlessly.

### 🧪 What I’ve Tried

Previously, Eliza supported an endpoint where I could send a message to a specific agent via `agentId` and get a response. That endpoint seems to no longer exist.

Here’s what I tried instead:

1.  Created a server and added both agents to it.
2.  Created a channel in that server and added both agents to the channel.
3.  Used the endpoint:
    `/api/messaging/central-channels/:channelId/messages`
    to forward the message.

### ⚠️ Issue

-   The support agent takes time to respond.
-   To check the response, I use:
    `/api/messaging/central-channels/:channelId/messages`
    But this requires polling, which isn’t reliable — sometimes the agent never responds or the polling misses it.

### ❓ Question

Is there a way to forward a message from one agent to another and **get a reliable response** without polling? Maybe a webhook, callback, or a newer approach?6:Tb08,Steps to reproduce:

8:T4a0,**Describe the bug**

<!-- A clear and concise description of what the bug is. -->
The GOAT plugin https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-goat doesnt work after eliza separation of plugins .

**To Reproduce**

<!-- Steps to reproduce the behavior. -->
```
1. Install the goat plugin in current stable version of elizaos -> npx elizaos plugins install @elizaos-plugins/plugin-goat
2. Add "plugins": ["@elizaos-plugins/plugin-goat"] to the characterfile and provide appropriate secrets.
3. Run the eliza with the character file. Even though the plugin is activated, the actions are not triggered.

```

The reason i found was with GOAT plugin returning a async function instead of a json structure.
https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-goat/blob/main/src/index.ts#L5.

Because of this https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/main/agent/src/index.ts#L390 the current elizaos logic doesnt handle this plugin properly.
This type of async exported plugins can work if in eliza, the code changes to 
```
await importedPlugin.default(secrets) instead of importedPlugin.default in above mentioned line L390
```

**Expected behavior**

Plugins with Async exported functions has to work.

a:Ta60,# Error when sending message without `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`: "x-api-key header is required"

## Description
When installing Eliza from source and running `bun start`, attempts to send a message to the agent fail with an `AI_APICallError: x-api-key header is required` error if the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` environment variable is not set. This occurs regardless of whether an `OPENAI_API_KEY` is present.

## Steps to Reproduce
1. Clone the Eliza repository and install dependencies.
2. Ensure only the `OPENAI_API_KEY` (or no API keys) is set in your environment; do not set `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.
3. From the project root, run:
   ```bash
   bun start
   ```
4. Attempt to send a message to the “Eliza” agent (e.g., via an HTTP request or CLI).
5. Observe the error in logs.

## Expected Behavior
- If `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is unset, Eliza should either:
  - Fall back to OpenAI if `OPENAI_API_KEY` is provided, or
  - Emit a warning about missing Anthropic functionality without throwing an error when handling messages.

## Actual Behavior
Eliza logs warnings about missing instrumentation and missing `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, then errors out on any message send with:
```text
(AI_APICallError) x-api-key header is required
at new _AISDKError (/eliza/node_modules/@ai-sdk/provider/dist/index.mjs:19:5)
...
```

## Relevant Logs
```text
[2025-04-23 22:20:42] WARN: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is not set in environment - Anthropic functionality will be limited
[2025-04-23 22:20:47] WARN: Service instrumentation not found
    agentName: "Eliza"
    agentId: "b850bc30-45f8-0041-a00a-83df46d8555d"
[2025-04-23 22:20:47] WARN: [getTracer] Service instrumentation not found in runtime.
[2025-04-23 22:20:48] ERROR: Error during emitEvent for MESSAGE_RECEIVED (handler execution):
    agentName: "Eliza"
    agentId: "b850bc30-45f8-0041-a00a-83df46d8555d"
    message: "(AI_APICallError) x-api-key header is required"
    stack: [
      "AI_APICallError: x-api-key header is required",
      "at new _AISDKError (/eliza/node_modules/@ai-sdk/provider/dist/index.mjs:19:5)",
      "at new APICallError (/eliza/node_modules/@ai-sdk/provider/dist/index.mjs:61:5)",
      "at <anonymous> (/eliza/node_modules/@ai-sdk/provider-utils/dist/index.mjs:667:18)",
      "at processTicksAndRejections (native:7:39)"
    ]
```

## Environment
- OS: Ubuntu 22.04 LTS
- Runtime: Bun v0.x
- Eliza Version: Source checkout as of April 23, 2025
- Env Vars:
  - `OPENAI_API_KEY`: set or unset
  - `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`: **not** set

## Additional Context
This issue blocks users who wish to run Eliza exclusively with OpenAI credentials. A fallback or clearer error message would improve usability.

b:T14a4,## 📚 Documentation Overhaul Proposal

### Background

We've received valuable feedback from a developer building on ElizaOS that our current documentation, while comprehensive, doesn't effectively serve our two distinct user groups. After using the product, they found it much improved since early 2024, but the documentation doesn't reflect this quality.

### The Problem

1. **Mixed Audiences**: Current docs blend beginner and advanced concepts without clear separation
2. **Missing Technical Details**: Core concepts like state management, sockets, and architecture decisions aren't well explained
3. **Unclear Guidance**: No clear direction on when to use standalone projects vs monorepo development
4. **Accessibility**: Non-technical users ("vibecoders") struggle to get started quickly

### Proposed Solution

Create a **two-track documentation system** that serves both audiences effectively:

## 🎯 Track 1: Simple Docs (For Vibecoders)

**Goal**: Get non-technical users running agents in 5 minutes without understanding internals

### Content Structure:
- **Quick Start**: One-page guide to go from zero to running agent
- **Templates Gallery**: Pre-built agents for common use cases
  - Discord bot template
  - Telegram assistant template
  - Trading bot template
  - Customer service agent template
- **Simple Customization**: 
  - How to change personality traits
  - Adding basic knowledge
  - Enabling/disabling features
- **Visual Guides**: Screenshots and videos for every step
- **FAQ**: Common issues and simple solutions

### Key Principles:
- Hide complexity
- Focus on outcomes, not process
- Provide copy-paste solutions
- Use `elizaos create` workflow exclusively

## 🔧 Track 2: Technical Documentation (For Developers)

**Goal**: Provide deep technical understanding for developers building on or contributing to ElizaOS

### Content Structure:

#### Architecture Deep Dive
- System architecture overview with diagrams
- Data flow and component interactions
- Design decisions and rationale

#### Core Concepts (Currently Missing\!)
- **State Management**: How agent memory, context, and state work
- **Room/World Abstraction**: Understanding the UUID system and agent perspectives
- **Socket Architecture**: Real-time communication implementation
- **Plugin Lifecycle**: From creation to deployment
- **Service Layer**: How services interact and maintain state

#### Development Guides
- **Monorepo vs Standalone**: Clear decision matrix
  - Standalone: For running agents, trying plugins, basic customization
  - Monorepo: For core development, custom plugins, contributing
- **Plugin Development**: Step-by-step guide with best practices
- **Testing Strategies**: Unit, integration, and e2e testing approaches
- **Performance Optimization**: Profiling and tuning guides

#### API Reference
- Complete API documentation
- TypeScript interfaces and types
- Code examples for every endpoint

## 🚀 Implementation Plan

### Phase 1: Structure & Navigation (Week 1-2)
1. Create new documentation structure with clear separation
2. Design interactive landing page with user journey paths
3. Set up navigation for both tracks

### Phase 2: Simple Docs (Week 3-4)
1. Write streamlined quick start guide
2. Create template gallery with 5-10 ready-to-use agents
3. Develop visual guides and screenshots
4. Build comprehensive FAQ

### Phase 3: Technical Docs (Week 5-8)
1. Document architecture with diagrams
2. Write deep dives on state, sockets, and core concepts
3. Create comprehensive plugin development guide
4. Build API reference documentation

### Phase 4: Enhancements (Week 9-10)
1. Add interactive examples
2. Create video tutorials
3. Implement search functionality
4. Add feedback mechanism

d:T1b10,**Issue Title**: `feat(scenarios): Implement final judgment and user-facing reports`

**Tags**: `cli`, `scenarios`, `feature`, `reporting`

#### **Description**

This is the final ticket to complete the core functionality of the Scenario Runner. Its purpose is to transition from printing raw development objects to producing a clean, polished, human-readable report for the user.

This involves two key pieces of work:

1. **Judgment Logic**: Implementing the logic defined in the `judgment` block of the scenario file. This will aggregate the individual results from the Evaluation Engine into a single, final `PASS` or `FAIL` outcome for the entire scenario.
2. **Reporting**: Creating a dedicated `Reporter` class to format and display all information about the run—from setup to execution to final results—in a structured and aesthetically pleasing way in the console.

The outcome of this ticket will be a production-ready user experience for the `elizaos scenario run` command.

#### **Acceptance Criteria**

1. A new `Reporter` class is created in `packages/cli/src/scenarios/Reporter.ts`.
2. The `handleRunScenario` function is refactored to use the `Reporter` for all console output, removing all intermediate `console.log` statements.
3. The logic for the `judgment.strategy` is implemented. For `all_pass`, the scenario only passes if every single evaluation result is `success: true`.
4. A final `SCENARIO STATUS` of `✅ PASS` or `❌ FAIL` is calculated and displayed at the end of the report.
5. The CLI process exits with code `0` if the final scenario status is `PASS`.
6. The CLI process exits with code `1` if the final scenario status is `FAIL`.
7. The final console output is well-structured, using headers, indentation, and status icons (e.g., ✅, ❌, ℹ️) for clarity.

#### **Technical Approach**

**1. Create the** `Reporter` Class

This class will manage all `stdout`. Using a dedicated class makes it easy to change the reporting format in the future without altering the core runner logic.

* **Create file**: `packages/cli/src/scenarios/Reporter.ts`

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/Reporter.ts
import { Scenario } from './schema';
import { ExecutionResult } from './providers';
import { EvaluationResult } from './EvaluationEngine';
import chalk from 'chalk'; // Add chalk for colored output: bun add chalk
export class Reporter {
  
  public reportStart(scenario: Scenario) {
    console.log(chalk.bold.cyan(`\n▶️ RUNNING SCENARIO: ${scenario.name}`));
    console.log(chalk.gray(`  ${scenario.description}\n`));
  }
  public reportExecutionResult(result: ExecutionResult) {
    console.log(chalk.bold('--- Execution Output ---'));
    if (result.stdout) {
      console.log(chalk.green('STDOUT:'));
      console.log(result.stdout.trim().split('\n').map(l => `  | ${l}`).join('\n'));
    }
    if (result.stderr) {
      console.log(chalk.yellow('STDERR:'));
      console.log(result.stderr.trim().split('\n').map(l => `  | ${l}`).join('\n'));
    }
    console.log(chalk.bold('------------------------\n'));
  }
  public reportEvaluationResults(results: EvaluationResult[]) {
    console.log(chalk.bold('--- Evaluation Results ---'));
    if (results.length === 0) {
      console.log(chalk.gray('  No evaluations were run.'));
    }
    results.forEach(res => {
      const status = res.success 
        ? chalk.green('✅ PASS') 
        : chalk.red('❌ FAIL');
      console.log(`${status}: ${res.message}`);
    });
    console.log(chalk.bold('------------------------\n'));
  }
  public reportFinalResult(finalSuccess: boolean) {
    const finalStatus = finalSuccess
      ? chalk.bold.green('✅ PASS')
      : chalk.bold.red('❌ FAIL');
    
    console.log(chalk.bold.cyan(`SCENARIO STATUS: ${finalStatus}`));
  }
}
```

**2. Integrate Judgment and Reporting into the CLI Command**

Refactor the main handler to use the `Reporter` and implement the final judgment logic.

* **Modify file**: `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts`

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts
// ... imports
import { Reporter } from '../scenarios/Reporter';
// ...
async function handleRunScenario(args: ScenarioRunArgs, runtime: AgentRuntime) {
  // --- SETUP ---
  const reporter = new Reporter();
  let finalStatus = false; // Default to fail
  
  // ... (file reading and validation) ...
  const scenario: Scenario = validationResult.data;
  reporter.reportStart(scenario);
  const provider = /* ... provider init ... */;
  const mockEngine = /* ... mock engine init ... */;
  const evaluationEngine = /* ... evaluation engine init ... */;
  try {
    // --- MOCKING & SETUP ---
    mockEngine.applyMocks(scenario.setup?.mocks);
    await provider.setup(scenario);
    // --- EXECUTION ---
    const execResult = await provider.run(scenario);
    reporter.reportExecutionResult(execResult);
    // --- EVALUATION ---
    const evalResults = await evaluationEngine.runEvaluations(scenario.run[0].evaluations, execResult);
    reporter.reportEvaluationResults(evalResults);
    // --- JUDGMENT ---
    if (scenario.judgment.strategy === 'all_pass') {
      finalStatus = evalResults.every(res => res.success);
    } else {
      // Future strategies like 'any_pass' would go here
      finalStatus = false;
    }
  } catch(error: any) {
    console.error(chalk.red(`\nFATAL ERROR: An unrecoverable error occurred during the scenario run.`), error);
    finalStatus = false;
  } finally {
    // --- CLEANUP & FINAL REPORT ---
    mockEngine.revertMocks();
    await provider.teardown();
    reporter.reportFinalResult(finalStatus);
    
    // Set exit code for CI/CD environments
    process.exit(finalStatus ? 0 : 1);
  }
}
```

     * Check the exit code: `echo $?` should return `0`.e:T277b,**Issue Title**: `feat(scenarios): Implement evaluation engine and basic evaluators`

**Tags**: `cli`, `scenarios`, `feature`, `evaluation`

#### **Description**

This ticket implements the core validation logic of the Scenario Runner: the **Evaluation Engine**. After a scenario's `run` block completes, this engine is responsible for executing a series of assertions (`evaluations`) to determine if the agent's actions and response meet the success criteria.

The work involves creating a generic `EvaluationEngine` that can manage and execute different types of evaluators. We will also implement the initial set of essential evaluators, covering checks against the agent's text response, the state of the execution environment's file system, the agent's internal action history, and even using an LLM as a judge for more nuanced assessments.

#### **Acceptance Criteria**

1. A new `EvaluationEngine` class and a generic `Evaluator` interface are created in `packages/cli/src/scenarios/EvaluationEngine.ts`.
2. The `EvaluationEngine` maintains a registry of all available evaluator types.
3. The following evaluators are implemented and registered with the engine:
   * `string_contains`
   * `regex_match`
   * `file_exists`
   * `trajectory_contains_action`
   * `llm_judge`
4. The main `handleRunScenario` function uses the `EvaluationEngine` to run all evaluations defined in the scenario after the `run` block completes.
5. The results of each evaluation (pass/fail status and a descriptive message) are collected and printed clearly to the console.
6. **Dependency**: The `ExecutionResult` object returned by `EnvironmentProvider` must be enhanced to include a representation of the final file system state, which is required for the `file_exists` evaluator.
7. **Dependency**: The `AgentRuntime` must provide a method to access the agent's action history (e.g., `runtime.getActionHistory()`), which is required for the `trajectory_contains_action` evaluator.

#### **Technical Approach**

**1. Enhance** `EnvironmentProvider` Contract

The `file_exists` evaluator needs to know about files in the environment *after* the run. We must update the provider interface to support this.

* **Modify file**: `packages/cli/src/scenarios/providers.ts`

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/providers.ts
export interface ExecutionResult {
  exitCode: number;
  stdout: string;
  stderr: string;
  // NEW: A map of all file paths to their string content within the execution environment.
  files: Record<string, string>; 
}
```

*Note: The* `LocalEnvironmentProvider` and `E2BEnvironmentProvider` tickets must be updated to implement this contract, recursively reading all files from their respective environments after execution.

**2. Define Evaluator Interfaces and Engine**

Create the core engine and the contracts that all evaluators must follow.

* **Create file**: `packages/cli/src/scenarios/EvaluationEngine.ts`

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/EvaluationEngine.ts
import { AgentRuntime } from '@elizaos/core';
import { ExecutionResult } from './providers';
import { Evaluation as EvaluationSchema } from './schema'; // Zod schema type
export interface EvaluationResult {
  success: boolean;
  message: string;
}
export interface Evaluator {
  evaluate(
    params: EvaluationSchema,
    runResult: ExecutionResult,
    runtime: AgentRuntime
  ): Promise<EvaluationResult>;
}
export class EvaluationEngine {
  private evaluators = new Map<string, Evaluator>();
  constructor(private runtime: AgentRuntime) {
    // Register all known evaluators
    this.register('string_contains', new StringContainsEvaluator());
    this.register('regex_match', new RegexMatchEvaluator());
    this.register('file_exists', new FileExistsEvaluator());
    this.register('trajectory_contains_action', new TrajectoryContainsActionEvaluator());
    this.register('llm_judge', new LLMJudgeEvaluator(this.runtime));
  }
  
  private register(type: string, evaluator: Evaluator) {
    this.evaluators.set(type, evaluator);
  }
  public async runEvaluations(
    evaluations: EvaluationSchema[],
    runResult: ExecutionResult
  ): Promise<EvaluationResult[]> {
    const results: EvaluationResult[] = [];
    for (const evaluation of evaluations) {
      const evaluator = this.evaluators.get(evaluation.type);
      if (!evaluator) {
        results.push({ success: false, message: `Unknown evaluator type: '${evaluation.type}'` });
        continue;
      }
      const result = await evaluator.evaluate(evaluation, runResult, this.runtime);
      results.push(result);
    }
    return results;
  }
}
// --- IMPLEMENTATIONS ---
// In the same file for simplicity, or can be broken out.
class StringContainsEvaluator implements Evaluator {
  async evaluate(params, runResult): Promise<EvaluationResult> {
    if (params.type !== 'string_contains') throw new Error('Mismatched evaluator');
    const success = runResult.stdout.includes(params.value);
    return {
      success,
      message: `Checked if stdout contains "${params.value}". Result: ${success}`,
    };
  }
}
class RegexMatchEvaluator implements Evaluator {
  async evaluate(params, runResult): Promise<EvaluationResult> {
    if (params.type !== 'regex_match') throw new Error('Mismatched evaluator');
    const success = new RegExp(params.pattern).test(runResult.stdout);
    return {
      success,
      message: `Checked if stdout matches regex "${params.pattern}". Result: ${success}`,
    };
  }
}
class FileExistsEvaluator implements Evaluator {
  async evaluate(params, runResult): Promise<EvaluationResult> {
    if (params.type !== 'file_exists') throw new Error('Mismatched evaluator');
    // Assumes 'file_exists' schema has a 'path' property
    const success = Object.keys(runResult.files).includes(params.path);
    return {
      success,
      message: `Checked if file "${params.path}" exists. Result: ${success}`,
    };
  }
}
class TrajectoryContainsActionEvaluator implements Evaluator {
  async evaluate(params, runResult, runtime): Promise<EvaluationResult> {
    if (params.type !== 'trajectory_contains_action') throw new Error('Mismatched evaluator');
    // Assumes runtime has a method to get action history
    const history = await runtime.getActionHistory(); // This method needs to be implemented on AgentRuntime
    const success = history.some(event => event.action === params.action);
    return {
      success,
      message: `Checked if action trajectory contains "${params.action}". Result: ${success}`,
    };
  }
}
class LLMJudgeEvaluator implements Evaluator {
  constructor(private runtime: AgentRuntime) {}
  async evaluate(params, runResult): Promise<EvaluationResult> {
    if (params.type !== 'llm_judge') throw new Error('Mismatched evaluator');
    const fullPrompt = `${params.prompt}\n\nAgent Response:\n${runResult.stdout}\n\nWas the expectation of "${params.expected}" met? Respond with only "yes" or "no".`;
    const response = await this.runtime.llm.prompt(fullPrompt); // Assumes runtime.llm.prompt exists
    const success = response.toLowerCase().includes(params.expected.toLowerCase());
    return {
      success,
      message: `LLM Judge assessment for prompt "${params.prompt}". Result: ${success}`,
    };
  }
}
```

*Note: The schema file (*`schema.ts`) will also need to be updated to include definitions for `file_exists` and its `path` parameter.

3. **Run the test** and verify the console output. The `Evaluation Results` block should show a clear `PASS` or `FAIL` status for each of the six evaluations, matching the expected outcomes defined in the test case.f:T20af,**Issue Title**: `feat(scenarios): Implement mock engine for service calls`

**Tags**: `cli`, `scenarios`, `feature`, `mocking`, `services`

#### **Description**

This ticket introduces one of the most critical features of the Scenario Runner: the ability to mock service method calls. This allows us to isolate the agent's reasoning and planning capabilities from the external services it depends on. By replacing live API calls with deterministic, predefined responses, we can create fast, reliable, and repeatable tests that can run in any environment without side effects.

The core task is to develop a `MockEngine` that intercepts method calls on services requested via `runtime.getService()`. It will check if a mock is defined in the scenario's `setup.mocks` array for that specific call. If a match is found, it returns the mocked response; otherwise, it allows the original method to execute. This engine must also support conditional mocking based on the arguments passed to the method.

#### **Acceptance Criteria**

1. A new `MockEngine` class is created in `packages/cli/src/scenarios/MockEngine.ts`.
2. The `handleRunScenario` function uses the `MockEngine` to apply mocks from the `setup.mocks` array before the `run` block is executed.
3. When a scenario with a simple mock is run, calls to the specified service and method are intercepted, and the predefined `response` is returned to the agent's code. The original service method is **not** called.
4. When a scenario uses a mock with a `when` clause, the mock is only applied if the arguments passed to the method deep-equal the arguments specified in the `when` clause.
5. If a call is made that does *not* match a `when` clause, the call proceeds to the original, un-mocked service method.
6. All mocks are cleanly reverted and removed after the scenario run completes (either successfully or in failure), ensuring no state leaks between tests.

#### **Technical Approach**

The most robust and least intrusive way to implement this is by decorating the `AgentRuntime` instance at runtime. We will use a `Proxy`-based approach to wrap services as they are requested, which allows for transparent interception of method calls.

**1. Create the** `MockEngine`

This class will encapsulate all mocking logic. We'll use the `lodash` library for reliable deep equality checks.

* **Add dependency**: `bun add lodash @types/lodash`
* **Create file**: `packages/cli/src/scenarios/MockEngine.ts`

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/MockEngine.ts
import { AgentRuntime } from '@elizaos/core';
import { Scenario } from './schema';
import _ from 'lodash';
type MockDefinition = NonNullable<NonNullable<Scenario['setup']>['mocks']>[0];
export class MockEngine {
  private originalGetService: AgentRuntime['getService'];
  constructor(private runtime: AgentRuntime) {
    this.originalGetService = this.runtime.getService.bind(this.runtime);
  }
  public applyMocks(mocks: MockDefinition[] = []) {
    if (mocks.length === 0) return;
    // A map to store mocks for efficient lookup. Key: "serviceName.methodName"
    const mockRegistry = new Map<string, MockDefinition[]>();
    for (const mock of mocks) {
      const key = `${mock.service}.${mock.method}`;
      if (!mockRegistry.has(key)) {
        mockRegistry.set(key, []);
      }
      mockRegistry.get(key)!.push(mock);
    }
    // Replace the original getService with our mocked version
    this.runtime.getService = <T>(name: string): T => {
      const originalService = this.originalGetService<T>(name);
      
      // Return a proxy for the service. This proxy intercepts all method calls.
      return new Proxy(originalService as any, {
        get: (target, prop: string, receiver) => {
          const key = `${name}.${prop}`;
          
          if (!mockRegistry.has(key)) {
            // No mock for this method, return the original.
            return Reflect.get(target, prop, receiver);
          }
          // Return a new function that will perform the mock logic
          return (...args: any[]) => {
            const potentialMocks = mockRegistry.get(key)!;
            
            // Find a conditional mock that matches the arguments
            const conditionalMock = potentialMocks.find(m => 
              m.when && _.isEqual(args, m.when.args) // Assumes 'when' has an 'args' array
            );
            if (conditionalMock) {
              return Promise.resolve(conditionalMock.response);
            }
            // Find a generic (non-conditional) mock
            const genericMock = potentialMocks.find(m => !m.when);
            if (genericMock) {
              return Promise.resolve(genericMock.response);
            }
            // No matching mock found, call the original method
            return Reflect.get(target, prop, receiver)(...args);
          };
        },
      }) as T;
    };
  }
  public revertMocks() {
    // Restore the original getService method to clean up.
    this.runtime.getService = this.originalGetService;
  }
}
```

**2. Update the** `when` clause in the Schema

The schema needs to be slightly more specific about the shape of the `when` clause.

* **Modify file**: `packages/cli/src/scenarios/schema.ts`

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/schema.ts
// ... (imports)
const MockSchema = z.object({
  service: z.string(),
  method: z.string(),
  // The 'when' clause specifically checks arguments now.
  when: z.object({
    args: z.array(z.any())
  }).optional(),
  response: z.any(),
});
// ... (rest of the file)
```

**3. Integrate into the CLI Command Handler**

Wire the `MockEngine` into the main execution flow.

* **Modify file**: `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts`

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts
// ... imports
import { MockEngine } from '../scenarios/MockEngine';
// ...
async function handleRunScenario(args: ScenarioRunArgs, runtime: AgentRuntime) {
  // ... (file reading and validation logic)
  const scenario: Scenario = validationResult.data;
  
  const provider = /* ... provider initialization ... */;
  const mockEngine = new MockEngine(runtime); // Create instance
  try {
    elizaLogger.info('Applying mocks...');
    mockEngine.applyMocks(scenario.setup?.mocks); // Apply mocks
    elizaLogger.info(`Setting up '${scenario.environment.type}' environment...`);
    await provider.setup(scenario);
    elizaLogger.info('Executing run block...');
    const result = await provider.run(scenario);
    // ... print execution result ...
  } catch(error) {
    elizaLogger.error('An error occurred during scenario execution:', error);
  } finally {
    elizaLogger.info('Reverting mocks and tearing down environment...');
    mockEngine.revertMocks(); // Crucial cleanup step
    await provider.teardown();
  }
}
```

**Tags**: `cli`, `scenarios`, `feature`, `environment-provider`, `e2b`

#### **Description**

This ticket integrates our most critical execution target: the E2B secure sandbox. The work involves creating an `E2BEnvironmentProvider` that acts as a client to the existing `@elizaos/plugin-e2b`.

Unlike the `LocalEnvironmentProvider`, this provider will not implement sandbox logic itself. Instead, it will delegate all sandbox operations (creation, file writing, command execution, and teardown) to the `e2b` service available within the `AgentRuntime`. This ensures a clean separation of concerns, where the Scenario Runner is simply a *client* of the sandbox infrastructure provided by the plugin.

A key part of this task is updating the command handler to be "runtime-aware," allowing it to access the necessary services to instantiate the correct provider.

#### **Acceptance Criteria**

1. A new `packages/cli/src/scenarios/E2BEnvironmentProvider.ts` file is created which implements the `EnvironmentProvider` interface.
2. The main `handleRunScenario` function is refactored to accept the `AgentRuntime` instance as an argument.
3. When a scenario specifies `environment: { type: 'e2b' }`, the runner attempts to fetch the `e2b` service from the runtime.
4. If the `e2b` service is not found, the runner exits with a clear error message instructing the user to install the plugin and configure their API key.
5. The `E2BEnvironmentProvider` uses the `e2b` service to create a sandbox during `setup`.
6. The provider correctly implements `setup.virtual_fs` by calling `e2bService.writeFileToSandbox()` for each specified file.
7. The provider executes the `run.input` command in the sandbox and correctly maps the result (`stdout`, `stderr`, `exitCode`) to the `ExecutionResult` object.
8. The provider properly terminates the sandbox during `teardown` by calling `e2bService.killSandbox()`.
9. The implementation respects the `E2B_API_KEY` and `E2B_MODE` environment variables as documented by the `plugin-e2b`.

#### **Technical Approach**

**1. Define the** `E2BService` Interface

Based on the `plugin-e2b` documentation, we can define a type for the service we expect to receive from the runtime. This helps with type safety.

* **Create/Modify file**: `packages/cli/src/scenarios/providers.ts`

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/providers.ts
// ... (existing interfaces)
/**
 * Defines the shape of the E2B service we expect from the runtime.
 * Based on the @elizaos/plugin-e2b documentation.
 */
export interface E2BService {
  createSandbox(config: { template?: string; timeoutMs?: number }): Promise<string>; // Returns sandboxId
  runCommand(sandboxId: string, command: string, rootdir?: string): Promise<{ stdout: string; stderr: string; exitCode: number }>;
  writeFileToSandbox(sandboxId: string, path: string, content: string): Promise<void>;
  killSandbox(sandboxId: string): Promise<void>;
}
```

**2. Implement the** `E2BEnvironmentProvider`

This class will contain all the logic for interacting with the E2B plugin.

* **Create file**: `packages/cli/src/scenarios/E2BEnvironmentProvider.ts`

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/E2BEnvironmentProvider.ts
import { EnvironmentProvider, ExecutionResult, E2BService } from './providers';
import { Scenario } from '../scenarios/schema';
import { AgentRuntime } from '@elizaos/core';
export class E2BEnvironmentProvider implements EnvironmentProvider {
  private runtime: AgentRuntime;
  private e2bService: E2BService;
  private sandboxId: string | null = null;
  constructor(runtime: AgentRuntime) {
    this.runtime = runtime;
    // Attempt to get the service immediately to fail fast.
    const service = this.runtime.getService<E2BService>('e2b');
    if (!service) {
      throw new Error(
        "E2BEnvironmentProvider required, but 'e2b' service was not found in runtime. " +
        "Please ensure @elizaos/plugin-e2b is installed and configured."
      );
    }
    this.e2bService = service;
  }
  async setup(scenario: Scenario): Promise<void> {
    this.sandboxId = await this.e2bService.createSandbox({});
    
    const virtualFs = scenario.setup?.virtual_fs;
    if (this.sandboxId && virtualFs) {
      for (const [filePath, content] of Object.entries(virtualFs)) {
        await this.e2bService.writeFileToSandbox(this.sandboxId, filePath, content);
      }
    }
  }
  async run(scenario: Scenario): Promise<ExecutionResult> {
    if (!this.sandboxId) {
      throw new Error('E2B sandbox not initialized. Call setup() first.');
    }
    const command = scenario.run[0].input;
    return await this.e2bService.runCommand(this.sandboxId, command);
  }
  async teardown(): Promise<void> {
    if (this.sandboxId) {
      await this.e2bService.killSandbox(this.sandboxId);
      this.sandboxId = null;
    }
  }
}
```

    process.exit(1);
  }
  
  // ... (the rest of the try/catch/finally block for setup/run/teardown is the same) ...

**Tags**: `cli`, `scenarios`, `feature`, `environment-provider`

#### **Description**

This ticket marks the transition from parsing scenarios to executing them. The primary goal is to build an extensible framework for running a scenario's `run` block within a specified environment. This will be achieved by creating an `EnvironmentProvider` interface that abstracts away the details of the execution target (e.g., local machine, E2B sandbox).

The first concrete implementation will be a `LocalEnvironmentProvider`, which executes the scenario's commands directly on the local machine using Node.js's built-in `child_process` and `fs` modules. To ensure clean, isolated runs, each scenario will be executed within a unique temporary directory that is created at the start of the run and destroyed at the end.

#### **Acceptance Criteria**

1. A new `packages/cli/src/scenarios/providers.ts` file is created, defining the `EnvironmentProvider` interface and related data structures (`ExecutionResult`, etc.).
2. A new `packages/cli/src/scenarios/LocalEnvironmentProvider.ts` file is created, which implements the `EnvironmentProvider` interface.
3. When a scenario with `environment: { type: 'local' }` is executed, the `LocalEnvironmentProvider` is instantiated and used.
4. The provider creates a temporary directory (e.g., in `/tmp/eliza-scenario-run-XXXXXX`) for the execution.
5. If `setup.virtual_fs` is defined, the specified files and their content are created within this temporary directory *before* the `run` block is executed.
6. The `run.input` command is executed with the temporary directory as its working directory.
7. The `stdout`, `stderr`, and `exitCode` from the executed command are captured in an `ExecutionResult` object.
8. After the execution is complete, the temporary directory and all its contents are deleted.
9. The main `handleRunScenario` function is updated to print the `ExecutionResult` to the console.

#### **Technical Approach**

**1. Define the Provider Interface**

First, we will formally define the contracts for any environment provider.

* **Create file**: `packages/cli/src/scenarios/providers.ts`

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/providers.ts
import { Scenario } from '../scenarios/schema';
/**
 * The result of executing the 'run' block in a scenario.
 */
export interface ExecutionResult {
  exitCode: number;
  stdout: string;
  stderr: string;
  // We may add more fields later, like file outputs.
}
/**
 * Defines the contract for an environment where a scenario can be executed.
 */
export interface EnvironmentProvider {
  /**
   * Prepares the environment for the run. This includes creating temporary
   * directories and seeding the file system.
   * @param scenario The full scenario definition object.
   */
  setup(scenario: Scenario): Promise<void>;
  /**
   * Executes the primary run command of the scenario.
   * @param scenario The full scenario definition object.
   */
  run(scenario: Scenario): Promise<ExecutionResult>;
  /**
   * Cleans up any resources created during the setup and run phases.
   */
  teardown(): Promise<void>;
}
```

**2. Implement the** `LocalEnvironmentProvider`

Next, we'll create the concrete implementation for local execution.

* **Create file**: `packages/cli/src/scenarios/LocalEnvironmentProvider.ts`

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/LocalEnvironmentProvider.ts
import {
  EnvironmentProvider,
  ExecutionResult,
} from './providers';
import { Scenario } from '../scenarios/schema';
import fs from 'fs/promises';
import path from 'path';
import os from 'os';
import { exec } from 'child_process';
import { promisify } from 'util';
const execAsync = promisify(exec);
export class LocalEnvironmentProvider implements EnvironmentProvider {
  private tempDir: string | null = null;
  async setup(scenario: Scenario): Promise<void> {
    const tempDirPrefix = path.join(os.tmpdir(), 'eliza-scenario-run-');
    this.tempDir = await fs.mkdtemp(tempDirPrefix);
    const virtualFs = scenario.setup?.virtual_fs;
    if (virtualFs) {
      for (const [filePath, content] of Object.entries(virtualFs)) {
        const fullPath = path.join(this.tempDir, filePath);
        await fs.mkdir(path.dirname(fullPath), { recursive: true });
        await fs.writeFile(fullPath, content);
      }
    }
  }
  async run(scenario: Scenario): Promise<ExecutionResult> {
    if (!this.tempDir) {
      throw new Error('Setup must be called before run.');
    }
    // For now, we only support the first run step.
    const command = scenario.run[0].input;
    try {
      const { stdout, stderr } = await execAsync(command, { cwd: this.tempDir });
      return { exitCode: 0, stdout, stderr };
    } catch (error: any) {
      // 'exec' throws an error for non-zero exit codes.
      return {
        exitCode: error.code || 1,
        stdout: error.stdout,
        stderr: error.stderr,
      };
    }
  }
  async teardown(): Promise<void> {
    if (this.tempDir) {
      await fs.rm(this.tempDir, { recursive: true, force: true });
      this.tempDir = null;
    }
  }
}
```

**3. Integrate into the CLI Command Handler**

Finally, update the main command logic to instantiate and use the provider.

* **Modify file**: `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts`

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts

import { EnvironmentProvider } from '../scenarios/providers';
// ... (command definition)

  // ... (file reading and validation logic from previous ticket)
  // This function now needs to be 'async'

  // --- NEW PROVIDER LOGIC ---
  let provider: EnvironmentProvider | null = null;
  if (scenario.environment.type === 'local') {
    provider = new LocalEnvironmentProvider();
  } else {
    elizaLogger.error(`Unsupported environment type: '${scenario.environment.type}'`);
    process.exit(1);
  }
  try {
    elizaLogger.info(`Setting up '${scenario.environment.type}' environment...`);
    await provider.setup(scenario);
    elizaLogger.info('Executing run block...');
    const result = await provider.run(scenario);
    console.log('--- Execution Result ---');
    console.log(JSON.stringify(result, null, 2));
    console.log('------------------------');
  } catch(error) {
    elizaLogger.error('An error occurred during scenario execution:', error);
  } finally {
    elizaLogger.info('Tearing down environment...');
    await provider.teardown();
  }
}
```

**Tags**: `cli`, `scenarios`, `feature`, `schema`

#### **Description**

This ticket builds directly upon the CLI scaffolding from the previous ticket. The goal is to move beyond simply printing the file contents and instead parse the scenario YAML into a fully-typed TypeScript object. This involves two key activities:

1. Defining the complete and canonical TypeScript interfaces for the entire `.scenario.yaml` schema.
2. Implementing robust runtime validation to ensure that any given scenario file strictly adheres to this schema, providing clear and actionable error messages for malformed files.

This will provide a safe and reliable foundation for the runner's execution logic.

#### **Acceptance Criteria**

1. A new file, `packages/cli/src/scenarios/schema.ts`, is created containing all the TypeScript `type` or `interface` definitions for the scenario structure.
2. The project adds `zod` as a dependency for runtime validation (`bun add zod`).
3. The `handleRunScenario` function from `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts` is updated to use `zod` to parse and validate the loaded YAML object against the defined schema.
4. If the YAML file is valid, the command prints the validated, type-safe JavaScript object to the console.
5. If the YAML file is invalid (e.g., missing required fields, incorrect data types), the command exits with a non-zero status code and prints a user-friendly error message detailing what is wrong and where.

#### **Technical Approach**

The core of this task is defining a robust schema and applying it. We will use the `zod` library for this, as it excels at parsing and validating unknown data structures and can infer TypeScript types directly from the schemas.

**1. Define the Zod Schema and TypeScript Types**

Create a new file for all schema-related definitions to keep the project organized.

* **Create file**: `packages/cli/src/scenarios/schema.ts`

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/schema.ts
import { z } from 'zod';
// Base schema for any evaluation
const BaseEvaluationSchema = z.object({
  type: z.string(),
});
// Specific evaluation schemas
const StringContainsEvaluationSchema = BaseEvaluationSchema.extend({
  type: z.literal('string_contains'),
  value: z.string(),
  case_sensitive: z.boolean().optional(),
});
const RegexMatchEvaluationSchema = BaseEvaluationSchema.extend({
  type: z.literal('regex_match'),
  pattern: z.string(),
});
const TrajectoryContainsActionSchema = BaseEvaluationSchema.extend({
  type: z.literal('trajectory_contains_action'),
  action: z.string(),
});
const LLMJudgeEvaluationSchema = BaseEvaluationSchema.extend({
  type: z.literal('llm_judge'),
  prompt: z.string(),
  expected: z.string(),
});
// A discriminated union allows Zod to figure out the correct schema based on the 'type' field
const EvaluationSchema = z.discriminatedUnion('type', [
  StringContainsEvaluationSchema,
  RegexMatchEvaluationSchema,
  TrajectoryContainsActionSchema,
  LLMJudgeEvaluationSchema,
  // Future evaluators (like file_exists) will be added here
]);
const MockSchema = z.object({
  service: z.string(),
  method: z.string(),
  when: z.record(z.any()).optional(), // Simple object for now
  response: z.any(),
});
const SetupSchema = z.object({
  mocks: z.array(MockSchema).optional(),
  virtual_fs: z.record(z.string()).optional(), // map of path -> content
  // db_seed will be added later
});
const RunStepSchema = z.object({
  name: z.string().optional(),
  input: z.string(),
  evaluations: z.array(EvaluationSchema),
});
const JudgmentSchema = z.object({
  strategy: z.enum(['all_pass', 'any_pass']),
});
// The master schema for the entire scenario file
export const ScenarioSchema = z.object({
  name: z.string(),
  description: z.string(),
  plugins: z.array(z.string()).optional(),
  environment: z.object({
    type: z.enum(['e2b', 'local']),
  }),
  setup: SetupSchema.optional(),
  run: z.array(RunStepSchema),
  judgment: JudgmentSchema,
});
// Infer the TypeScript type from the Zod schema
export type Scenario = z.infer<typeof ScenarioSchema>;
```

**2. Integrate Validation into the CLI Command**

Modify the command handler from the previous ticket to use our new schema.

* **Modify file**: `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts`

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts
import type { Argv } from 'yargs';
import yaml from 'js-yaml';
import fs from 'fs';
import path from 'path';
import { elizaLogger } from '@elizaos/core';
import { ScenarioSchema, type Scenario } from '../scenarios/schema'; // Import our new schema
// ... (interface and command definitions remain the same) ...
function handleRunScenario(args: ScenarioRunArgs) {
  elizaLogger.info(`Starting scenario run with args: ${JSON.stringify(args)}`);
  try {
    const fullPath = path.resolve(args.filePath);
    elizaLogger.info(`Attempting to read scenario file from: ${fullPath}`);
    if (!fs.existsSync(fullPath)) {
      elizaLogger.error(`Error: File not found at '${fullPath}'`);
      process.exit(1);
    }
    const fileContents = fs.readFileSync(fullPath, 'utf8');
    const rawScenario = yaml.load(fileContents);
    // --- NEW VALIDATION LOGIC ---
    const validationResult = ScenarioSchema.safeParse(rawScenario);
    if (!validationResult.success) {
      elizaLogger.error('Scenario file validation failed:');
      // Zod's error formatting is very readable
      console.error(JSON.stringify(validationResult.error.format(), null, 2));
      process.exit(1);
    }
    
    // The data is now guaranteed to be of type 'Scenario'
    const scenario: Scenario = validationResult.data;
    console.log('--- Validated Scenario Object ---');
    console.log(JSON.stringify(scenario, null, 2));
    console.log('-------------------------------');
    
    elizaLogger.info('Scenario file parsed and validated successfully.');
  } catch (error) {
    elizaLogger.error('An error occurred during scenario execution:', error);
    process.exit(1);
  }
}
```

This ticket creates the foundational entry point for the Scenario Runner within the ElizaOS CLI. The goal is to register a new top-level `elizaos scenario` command with a `run` subcommand. This command will be responsible for parsing command-line arguments and will serve as the starting point for all future scenario execution logic.

#### **Technical Approach**

The CLI application in `packages/cli` uses the `yargs` library to define and manage commands. We will follow the existing pattern to add our new command.

**1. Create the Command Handler File**

First, create a new file to encapsulate the logic for the `scenario` command.

* **Create file**: `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts`

**2. Implement the Command Logic**

Inside `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts`, we will define the command structure and its handler. We will also need to add a YAML parsing library.

* **Add dependency**: From the root of the `eliza` monorepo, run `bun add js-yaml` to make it available to the CLI package.

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts
import type { Argv } from 'yargs';
import yaml from 'js-yaml';
import fs from 'fs';
import path from 'path';
import { elizaLogger } from '@elizaos/core'; // Assuming a shared logger
// Define the arguments for the 'run' subcommand
interface ScenarioRunArgs {
  filePath: string;
  live: boolean;
}
// Command definition for 'scenario run'
export const command = 'scenario <command>';
export const desc = 'Manage and execute ElizaOS scenarios.';
export const builder = (yargs: Argv): Argv => {
  return yargs.command(
    'run <filePath>',
    'Execute a scenario from a YAML file.',
    (yargs: Argv) => {
      return yargs
        .positional('filePath', {
          describe: 'Path to the .scenario.yaml file',
          type: 'string',
          demandOption: true,
        })
        .option('live', {
          alias: 'l',
          type: 'boolean',
          description: 'Run scenario in live mode, ignoring mocks.',
          default: false,
        });
    },
    (argv: ScenarioRunArgs) => {
      // This is the handler function that executes when the command is called
      handleRunScenario(argv);
    }
  );
};
// The core logic for the command
function handleRunScenario(args: ScenarioRunArgs) {
  elizaLogger.info(`Starting scenario run with args: ${JSON.stringify(args)}`);
  try {
    const fullPath = path.resolve(args.filePath);
    elizaLogger.info(`Attempting to read scenario file from: ${fullPath}`);
    if (!fs.existsSync(fullPath)) {
      elizaLogger.error(`Error: File not found at '${fullPath}'`);
      process.exit(1);
    }
    const fileContents = fs.readFileSync(fullPath, 'utf8');
    const scenario = yaml.load(fileContents);
    console.log('--- Parsed Scenario Content ---');
    console.log(JSON.stringify(scenario, null, 2));
    console.log('-----------------------------');
    
    elizaLogger.info('Scenario file parsed successfully.');
  } catch (error) {
    elizaLogger.error('An error occurred during scenario execution:', error);
    process.exit(1);
  }
}
```

**3. Register the New Command**

Now, we need to wire our new command module into the main CLI entry point.

* **Modify file**: `packages/cli/src/cli.ts` (or the equivalent main `yargs` file where other commands are registered).

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/cli.ts
// ... other imports
import * as scenario from './commands/scenario'; // Import the new module
// ... inside the main yargs setup function
// Find where other .command() calls are made, and add this one.
// It will likely be chained with the others.
yargs
  .command(agent)
  .command(test)
  .command(scenario) // Add our new scenario command module here
  // ... other commands and configurations
```

\### Summary

Windows CI tests are failing due to incorrect path handling when running \`elizaos agent start\` with the \`--path\` flag. The file path is being malformed, resulting in doubled/concatenated paths with embedded quotes.

\### Error Details

The test command:

\`\`\`bash

elizaos agent start --remote-url http://localhost:3000 --path "D:\\a\\eliza\\eliza\\packages\\cli\\tests\\test-characters\\max.json"

\`\`\`

Results in this malformed path:

\`\`\`

D:\\a\\eliza\\eliza\\packages\\cli\\"D:\\a\\eliza\\eliza\\packages\\cli\\tests\\test-characters\\max.json"

\`\`\`

Error message:

\`\`\`

error: File not found at path: D:\\a\\eliza\\eliza\\packages\\cli\\"D:\\a\\eliza\\eliza\\packages\\cli\\tests\\test-characters\\max.json"

\`\`\`

\### Root Cause

The path resolution in \`packages/cli/src/commands/agent.ts\` (around the \`startAgent\` function) is not properly handling quoted paths on Windows. When \`path.resolve(process.cwd(), options.path)\` is called, it's incorrectly concatenating the current working directory with an already-absolute path.

\### Affected Code

\- File: \`packages/cli/dist/chunk-KKAK7OQA.js\` (compiled from source)

\- Line: Around line 3326 where \`path12.resolve(process.cwd(), options.path)\` is called

\- Source likely in: \`packages/cli/src/commands/agent.ts\` or related agent command files

\### Reproduction

Only occurs on Windows systems when running:

\`\`\`bash

bun test packages/cli/tests/create-commands.test.ts

\`\`\`

\### Proposed Fix

1\. Check if the provided path is already absolute before calling \`path.resolve()\`

2\. Handle Windows path separators and quoted paths correctly

3\. Add unit tests specifically for Windows path scenarios

\### Example Fix

\`\`\`typescript

*// Instead of:*

const filePath = path.resolve(process.cwd(), options.path);

*// Use:*

const inputPath = options.path.replace(/^"|"$/g, ''); *// Remove surrounding quotes*

const filePath = path.isAbsolute(inputPath)

? inputPath

: path.resolve(process.cwd(), inputPath);

\`\`\`

\### Priority

High - Blocking CI/CD pipeline

\- bug

\- windows

\- cli

17:Te6f,\## Problem Details

\### Current Behavior

\- User runs \`elizaos create myproject\` from their desktop

\- The \`.elizadb\` directory gets created in the current directory (desktop) instead of inside \`myproject/\`

\- This causes database initialization issues for new projects

\### Expected Behavior

\- Database directory should be created at: \`myproject/.eliza/.elizadb/\`

\- All project-specific files should be contained within the new project directory

\## Root Cause Analysis

After analyzing the codebase, the issue is in how the \`resolvePgliteDir\` function determines the database directory during project creation:

1\. \*\*In \`packages/cli/src/commands/create/actions/creators.ts\`\*\*:

\- The \`createProject\` function creates the project directory but doesn't properly pass the target directory context for database initialization

\- Line 313-364: Project creation happens but database setup uses wrong context

2\. \*\*In \`packages/cli/src/utils/resolve-utils.ts\`\*\*:

\- Line 72-82: The function has logic to handle \`targetProjectDir\` but it's not being properly utilized

\- The fallback logic defaults to \`process.cwd()\` when it should use the new project directory

\## Proposed Fix

\### 1. Update \`createProject\` function to properly pass target directory:

\`\`\`typescript

*// In creators.ts*

await setupProjectEnvironment(projectTargetDir, database, aiModel, embeddingModel, true)

\`\`\`

\### 2. Ensure \`setupPgLite\` receives correct directory context:

\`\`\`typescript

*// In setup.ts*

export async function setupPgLite(

*dbDir*: string | undefined,

*envPath*: string | undefined,

*targetProjectDir*?: string

): Promise<void> {

const dirs = await ensureElizaDir(targetProjectDir);

*// ... rest of implementation*

}

\`\`\`

\### 3. Fix \`resolvePgliteDir\` to properly use target directory:

\`\`\`typescript

*// When targetProjectDir is provided, use it as the base*

const projectRoot = targetProjectDir || pathsInfo.monorepoRoot || process.cwd();

\`\`\`

\## Testing Requirements

1\. \*\*Manual Testing\*\*:

 \`\`\`bash

\# From any directory

elizaos create testproject

\# Verify .elizadb is inside testproject/.eliza/

ls testproject/.eliza/

 \`\`\`

2\. \*\*Automated Tests\*\*:

\- Add test in \`packages/cli/tests/commands/create.test.ts\`

\- Verify database directory is created in correct location

\- Test both interactive and non-interactive modes

3\. \*\*Edge Cases\*\*:

\- Creating project in current directory with \`elizaos create .\`

\- Creating project with absolute path

\- Creating project when parent has existing \`.eliza\` directory

\## Implementation Steps

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.ai/code)1b:T12db,## 🔧 Enhanced Module Resolution for Consistent Local-First Loading

This PR addresses critical module resolution issues by enhancing the existing ModuleLoader system to provide the same local-first guarantees as server-manager.ts, ensuring consistent module loading across all CLI operations.

### 🎯 Problem Statement

The CLI was experiencing inconsistent module resolution between different components:
- **server-start.ts** was using ModuleLoader but without explicit local-first guarantees
- **server-manager.ts** had proper local-first environment setup for spawned processes
- **Inconsistent behavior** between development and production environments
- **Potential singleton pattern issues** due to mixed global/local module loading

### 🚀 Key Changes

#### 1. **Enhanced ModuleLoader System** (`packages/cli/src/utils/module-loader.ts`)
- **NEW: Environment Setup**: Added `setupEnvironment()` method that mirrors server-manager.ts logic
- **NEW: Explicit Local Detection**: Checks if local module exists before resolution
- **NEW: Enhanced Logging**: Clear feedback about which installation is being used
- **IMPROVED: Validation**: Warns if expected local module resolves to global

#### 2. **Consistent Architecture**
- **ModuleLoader** → For in-process module loading with local-first guarantees
- **server-manager.ts** → For spawned process environment setup with local-first guarantees
- **Both systems** → Use same approach: check local existence, prioritize local, set environment variables

#### 3. **Environment Variable Management**
Enhanced ModuleLoader now sets up environment like server-manager.ts:
- `NODE_PATH = "{project}/node_modules:{existing_NODE_PATH}"`
- `PATH = "{project}/node_modules/.bin:{existing_PATH}"`

### 🧪 Testing and Validation

- ✅ **Build System**: All builds pass with enhanced ModuleLoader
- ✅ **Local Module Detection**: Correctly identifies and uses local @elizaos packages
- ✅ **Environment Setup**: Properly configures NODE_PATH and PATH variables
- ✅ **Logging**: Clear feedback about local vs global module usage
- ✅ **Backward Compatibility**: All existing APIs maintained

### 📈 Impact and Benefits

#### **Development Experience**
- **Consistent Behavior**: Same module resolution logic across all CLI operations
- **Local-First Approach**: Always prioritizes project's exact dependency versions
- **Clear Feedback**: Explicit logging about which installation is being used
- **Better Debugging**: Enhanced error messages with installation guidance

#### **Architecture Improvements**
- **Unified Strategy**: Both in-process and spawned processes use same local-first approach
- **Singleton Safety**: Prevents multiple instances of core modules
- **Environment Consistency**: Standardized module resolution across all operations
- **Maintainability**: Centralized local-first logic in ModuleLoader

### 🔍 Implementation Details

#### **Enhanced Module Loading Strategy**
ModuleLoader now includes explicit local-first checks:
```typescript
const localModulePath = path.join(this.projectPath, 'node_modules', moduleName);
const isLocalModule = existsSync(localModulePath);

if (isLocalModule) {
  logger.info(`Using local ${moduleName} installation`);
} else {
  logger.info(`Using global ${moduleName} installation`);
}
```

#### **Environment Enhancement (NEW)**
Added setupEnvironment() method:
```typescript
private setupEnvironment(): void {
  // Add local node_modules to NODE_PATH
  const localModulesPath = path.join(this.projectPath, 'node_modules');
  if (existsSync(localModulesPath)) {
    process.env.NODE_PATH = `${localModulesPath}${path.delimiter}${process.env.NODE_PATH || ''}`;
  }

  // Add local .bin to PATH
  const localBinPath = path.join(this.projectPath, 'node_modules', '.bin');
  if (existsSync(localBinPath)) {
    process.env.PATH = `${localBinPath}${path.delimiter}${process.env.PATH || ''}`;
  }
}
```

### 🛠 Technical Notes

- **Enhanced, Not Replaced**: ModuleLoader was enhanced rather than replaced
- **Consistent Approach**: Both ModuleLoader and server-manager.ts use same local-first logic
- **Environment Variables**: Proper PATH and NODE_PATH setup for consistent resolution
- **Explicit Validation**: Checks that local modules are actually being used when expected
- **Comprehensive Logging**: Clear feedback about module resolution decisions

### 📦 Files Changed

- `packages/cli/src/utils/module-loader.ts` - Enhanced with local-first guarantees
- `packages/cli/src/commands/start/actions/server-start.ts` - Uses enhanced ModuleLoader

21:T125c,# Fix: Plugin Actions Not Loading in NPM Deployed Version

## 🐛 Problem

Plugin actions were not being received by the runtime when using the NPM deployed version of the ElizaOS CLI. This issue only occurred with the published NPM package - everything worked correctly in the local monorepo development environment.

**Symptoms:**
- `elizaos create` followed by `elizaos start` with plugins would show 0 actions registered
- Simple REPLY worked but no plugin actions were available
- Bootstrap plugin reported "0 actions, 0 evaluators, 0 providers" despite having many

## 🔍 Root Cause Analysis

### 1. **Unresolved Workspace Dependencies**
The primary issue was that packages in the monorepo use `workspace:*` protocol for internal dependencies:
```json
{
  "dependencies": {
    "@elizaos/core": "workspace:*"
  }
}
```
When published to NPM, these workspace references were not resolved to actual version numbers, causing installation failures.

### 2. **Version Mismatches**
Build failures due to nx@19.8.15 requesting @nrwl packages that don't exist (they stopped at 19.8.14).

### 3. **Module Export Detection**
The plugin loader wasn't robust enough to handle different export patterns between development and production builds.

## ✅ Solution

### 1. **Dependency Resolution Script** (`prepare-packages-for-testing.sh`)
Created a script that resolves `workspace:*` to actual version numbers before packing:
```bash
# Replaces workspace:* with actual versions
sed -i.bak "s/\"workspace:\*\"/\"$VERSION\"/g" package.json
```

### 2. **Enhanced Plugin Export Detection** 
Improved the module export detection in `plugin-utils.ts` to handle multiple export patterns:
- Named export 'plugin'
- Default export
- Direct module export
- Expected function name pattern
- All exports as last resort

### 3. **Runtime Plugin Verification**
Added comprehensive logging to verify plugin registration:
```typescript
logger.info(`Registered ${registeredPlugins.length} plugins: ${registeredPlugins.join(', ')}`);
logger.info(`Registered ${registeredActions.length} actions: ${registeredActions.join(', ')}`);
```

### 4. **Version Resolution**
Fixed nx/nrwl version conflicts by adding resolutions in root `package.json`:
```json
"resolutions": {
  "nx": "19.8.14",
  "@nx/devkit": "19.8.14",
  "@nrwl/devkit": "19.8.14",
  "@nrwl/tao": "19.8.14"
}
```

### 5. **Testing Infrastructure**
Created comprehensive testing scripts to reproduce and debug NPM deployment locally:
- `test-cli-npm-pack.sh` - Tests exact NPM installation experience
- `debug-cli-plugin-loading.sh` - Debugging plugin loading issues
- `install-cli-globally.sh` - Install CLI globally from tarballs

## 📋 Changes Made

### Modified Files:
- `packages/cli/src/commands/start/utils/plugin-utils.ts` - Enhanced module export detection
- `packages/cli/src/commands/start/actions/agent-start.ts` - Added plugin verification logging
- `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/index.ts` - Added consistent named export
- `packages/plugin-bootstrap/tsup.config.ts` - Updated build configuration
- `packages/plugin-bootstrap/package.json` - Added peer dependencies
- `package.json` - Added dependency resolutions

### New Files:
- `scripts/prepare-packages-for-testing.sh` - Resolves workspace dependencies
- `scripts/test-cli-npm-pack.sh` - Tests NPM deployment locally
- `scripts/debug-cli-plugin-loading.sh` - Debug plugin loading
- `scripts/install-cli-globally.sh` - Global installation script
- `CLI_TESTING_GUIDE.md` - Comprehensive testing documentation
- `PLUGIN_LOADING_SOLUTION.md` - Root cause analysis and solution

## 🧪 Testing

### To verify the fix:
```bash
# Test NPM deployment locally
./scripts/test-cli-npm-pack.sh

# Or install globally and test
./scripts/install-cli-globally.sh
elizaos create test-project
cd test-project
elizaos start
```

### Expected Result:
```

Registered 71 actions: CONTINUE, GET_ROOM_FACTS, UPDATE_ROOM_FACTS...
```

This fix ensures that:
1. Plugins work correctly when installed from NPM
2. The development experience matches the production experience
3. Plugin authors can reliably test their plugins before publishing
4. Users get a working ElizaOS with all plugin capabilities

- Testing guide for CLI development

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.ai/code)23:Tde7,# Refactor: Complete Migration from JSON to XML Prompts

## Summary

This PR completes a comprehensive refactoring of all prompts across the ElizaOS codebase to use XML format instead of JSON, significantly improving LLM response reliability and consistency.

## Motivation

Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown better performance and more reliable parsing when working with XML format compared to JSON. This is particularly important for complex structured outputs where JSON parsing can be fragile due to:

- Escape character handling
- Nested object complexity
- String delimiters and special characters
- Inconsistent formatting across different LLM providers

## Changes Made

### Core Package Updates

- **entities.ts**: Updated entity resolution template to XML format for better entity matching

### Plugin-Bootstrap Actions Refactored

- **imageGeneration.ts**: Migrated image prompt generation to XML
- **reply.ts**: Updated reply template to use XML response format
- **sendMessage.ts**: Converted target extraction to XML structure
- **settings.ts**: Refactored all setting templates (success, failure, error, completion) to XML
- **updateEntity.ts**: Updated component data extraction to XML format
- **roles.ts**: Converted role assignment parsing to XML and removed `dedent` dependency
- **choice.ts**: Updated option extraction template to XML format

### Evaluators Refactored

- **reflection.ts**: Converted complex nested fact and relationship structures to XML with proper array handling

### Test Suite Updates

- **core/entities.test.ts**: Updated all entity tests to mock `parseKeyValueXml` instead of `parseJSONObjectFromText`
- **plugin-bootstrap/evaluators.test.ts**: Fixed reflection evaluator tests to handle XML responses
- **plugin-bootstrap/actions.test.ts**: Updated reply action tests to expect XML format

### Technical Improvements

1. **Consistent Parsing**: All responses now use `parseKeyValueXml` for uniform parsing
2. **Model Type Updates**: Changed from `OBJECT_LARGE/OBJECT_SMALL` to `TEXT_LARGE/TEXT_SMALL` where appropriate
3. **Better Structure Handling**: Improved handling of nested structures (arrays within XML)
4. **Type Safety**: Fixed TypeScript errors with proper type annotations
5. **Clear Instructions**: Added explicit prompting to avoid LLM reasoning sections in responses
6. **Dependency Cleanup**: Removed unnecessary `dedent` dependency from roles.ts

## XML Structure Examples

### Before (JSON):

```json
{
  "thought": "Analyzing the situation",
  "actions": ["REPLY", "UPDATE_ENTITY"],
  "text": "Hello, how can I help?"
}
```

### After (XML):

```xml
<response>
  <thought>Analyzing the situation</thought>
  <actions>REPLY,UPDATE_ENTITY</actions>
  <text>Hello, how can I help?</text>
</response>
```

- ✅ Cleaner, more maintainable code
- ✅ Improved test coverage with proper XML mocking

- Core entity tests now properly mock XML parsing
- Plugin-bootstrap tests updated to expect XML responses
- Test utilities enhanced to support both legacy and new formats

## Breaking Changes

None - This is an internal refactoring that maintains the same external API. All public interfaces remain unchanged.

2c:Td07,## Overview
Fixes several UX issues in the `elizaos monorepo` command to match the quality and behavior of other CLI commands.

## Bug Fixes

### 1. Graceful Cleanup on Interrupt
**Before:**
```bash
$ elizaos monorepo
Cloning into './eliza'...
^C
$ ls
eliza/  # ❌ Partial clone left behind
```

**After:**
```bash
$ elizaos monorepo
Cloning into './eliza'...
^C

Interrupted! Cleaning up monorepo...
Cleanup completed.
$ ls
# ✅ No directory left behind
```

### 2. Clean Error Messages for Invalid Branch
**Before:**
```bash
$ elizaos monorepo --branch invalid
[X] Branch 'invalid' doesn't exist in the ElizaOS repository.
Please specify a valid branch name. Common branches include:
  • main - The main branch
  • develop - The development branch (default)

For a complete list of branches, visit: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/branches
Error: Branch 'invalid' not found
    at cloneRepository (/path/to/clone.ts:37:13)
    at processTicksAndRejections... # Stack trace continues
```

**After:**
```bash
$ elizaos monorepo --branch invalid
error: Branch 'invalid' not found in the ElizaOS repository.
Available branches: main, develop
For a complete list, visit: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/branches
```

### 3. Empty Parameter Validation
**Before:**
```bash
$ elizaos monorepo --branch ""
Cloning into './eliza'...  # ❌ Proceeds with empty branch

$ elizaos monorepo --dir ""
Cloning into '/'...  # ❌ Dangerous behavior
```

**After:**
```bash
$ elizaos monorepo --branch ""
error: Branch name cannot be empty. Usage: elizaos monorepo --branch <branch-name>

$ elizaos monorepo --dir ""
error: Directory name cannot be empty. Usage: elizaos monorepo --dir <directory-name>
```

## Design Principles

- **Consistency**: Matches  best practices in`elizaos create` command patterns
- **ElizaOS Over Bun Run**: Prefers elizaos commands for starting server (start, dev) over bun commands
- **Fail Fast**: Validates inputs before any operations
- **Clean Errors**: User-friendly messages without stack traces
- **Safety**: Prevents partial states and validates all parameters

## Implementation Details

1. **Shared Cleanup Utility**: Extracted `withCleanupOnInterrupt` to `/packages/cli/src/utils/cleanup-on-interrupt.ts` for reuse
2. **Input Validation**: Added parameter validation before processing

## Summary

This PR refactors the Ollama plugin integration to make it truly conditional based on configuration, rather than being always included as a universal fallback. This change improves the flexibility of model selection and ensures Ollama is only loaded when explicitly configured or when no other LLM providers are available.

## Problem

Previously, the Ollama plugin was:

- Always included in the project-starter character configuration as a "universal fallback"
- Automatically set up during project creation regardless of user selection
- Hardcoded as a fallback even when users had configured other LLM providers

This caused issues where:

- Ollama would be unnecessarily loaded even when cloud-based LLMs were configured
- The plugin registration order could interfere with intended model precedence
- Users couldn't opt out of Ollama if they only wanted to use cloud providers

## Solution

### 1. **Conditional Ollama Loading in Character Configuration**

- Changed `packages/project-starter/src/character.ts` to only include the Ollama plugin when `OLLAMA_API_ENDPOINT` is configured
- Maintains proper plugin order: Core → LLM providers → Ollama (if configured) → Platform plugins → Bootstrap

### 2. **Removed Automatic Ollama Setup in CLI**

- Removed forced Ollama configuration from `packages/cli/src/commands/create/actions/creators.ts`
- No longer automatically sets up Ollama as a universal fallback during project creation

### 3. **Improved Model Selection Logic**

- Added `hasEmbeddingSupport()` helper function in `packages/cli/src/commands/create/utils/selection.ts`
- Identifies which AI models have built-in embedding support (local/Ollama, OpenAI, Google)
- Replaced hardcoded checks for `claude` and `openrouter` with the new helper function

### 4. **Consistent 'local' Model Mapping**

- Changed model mapping so 'local' consistently maps to the Ollama plugin
- Improved clarity in setup flow where 'local' means Ollama for both AI and embeddings

### 5. **Simplified Setup Flow**

- Removed redundant Ollama setup code from `setupAIModelConfig()`
- Consolidated embedding configuration logic
- Only installs Ollama plugin when explicitly selected or needed for embeddings

## Impact

- **Better Performance**: Ollama plugin only loads when needed, reducing unnecessary overhead
- **Clearer Intent**: Users explicitly choose when to use Ollama rather than having it forced
- **Improved Flexibility**: Easier to use cloud-only setups without local model fallbacks
- **Consistent Behavior**: All LLM provider plugins now follow the same conditional loading pattern

## Testing

- [x] Tested project creation with cloud-only providers (no Ollama loaded)
- [x] Tested project creation with 'local' option (Ollama properly configured)
- [x] Tested embedding model selection for providers without embedding support
- [x] Verified plugin registration order is maintained
- [x] Confirmed Ollama loads when `OLLAMA_API_ENDPOINT` is set in existing projects

## Breaking Changes

None. This change maintains backward compatibility:

- Existing projects with `OLLAMA_API_ENDPOINT` configured will continue to load the Ollama plugin
- Projects without the environment variable simply won't load the plugin
- The CLI create flow remains the same from a user perspective
2e:T77f,### PR Review – JSON B insert failure fix in `plugin-sql`

Great catch!  
The change that stringifies `content` and `metadata` before passing them to

```ts
sql`${...}::jsonb`
```

solves the root issue where raw objects were implicitly coerced to the string `"[object Object]"`.  
That coercion broke the `::jsonb` cast, triggered `Failed query` errors, and blocked service startup (most visibly with `plugin-twitter`).

#### What this patch does
1. **`createMemory`**
   * Detects string vs. object and always stores a *JSON string*:
     ```ts
     const contentToInsert  = typeof memory.content  === 'string'
                              ? memory.content
                              : JSON.stringify(memory.content ?? {});
     const metadataToInsert = typeof memory.metadata === 'string'
                              ? memory.metadata
                              : JSON.stringify(memory.metadata ?? {});
     ```
   * Uses those variables in the `INSERT`.

2. **`updateMemory`**
   * Mirrors the same logic when updating existing rows.

3. **Safety**
   * No DB-schema changes; only the way values are bound.
   * Back-compatible with records already stored as proper JSON.

#### Why this matters
* Prevents runtime-blocking SQL exceptions on any plugin that passes nested objects (Twitter, Discord, etc.).
* Makes the adapter resilient to future changes where callers send rich objects.
* Keeps the `::jsonb` cast (fast path for PG) without forcing every upstream plugin to remember to stringify.

#### Suggested follow-ups
* Add a small unit test for `createMemory`/`updateMemory` verifying that both object and string inputs land in Postgres as valid JSON.
* Consider centralising this stringify helper to avoid duplication if more fields become JSONB later.

Looks good – with this in, service registration succeeds and no more `[object Object]` inserts. 🎉2f:Tc91,## Problem

The `elizaos create` command had a few tiny UX issues:

1. Showed `bun run` commands instead of `elizaos` CLI commands
2. `--dir ""` was accepted without validation
3. `--type` flag didn't skip type selection
4. Validation errors showed full stack traces making them unreadable
5. Plugin names ending with "plugin" were silently truncated

## Solution

Improved validation, error handling, and user communication throughout the create command.

## Details

### 1. Fixed Next Steps Instructions
**Before:** `bun run build`, `bun run test`, `bun run dev`  
**After:** `elizaos test`, `elizaos dev`, `elizaos start` with descriptions

### 2. Added `--dir` Empty String Validation
```typescript
dir: z.string().min(1, 'Directory path cannot be empty').default('.'),
```

### 3. Fixed `--type` Flag Behavior
```typescript
const typeWasProvided = opts && 'type' in opts;
if (!isNonInteractive && !typeWasProvided) {
  // Show type selection only when --type not provided
}
```

### 4. Cleaned Up All Error Messages
**Before:** Full ZodError objects with stack traces (20+ lines)  
**After:** Single line errors like `error: Invalid dir: Directory path cannot be empty`

Fixed excessive output for:
- Invalid `--dir` value
- Invalid `--type` value  
- Existing directory errors
- Missing project name in `--yes` mode

**Implementation:**
```typescript
// Special handling for validation errors
if (error instanceof z.ZodError) {
  const firstError = error.errors[0];
  console.error(`error: Invalid ${firstError.path.join('.')}: ${firstError.message}`);
  process.exit(1);
}

// Show stack traces only in debug mode
if (error instanceof Error) {
  console.error(`error: ${error.message}`);
  if (process.env.LOG_LEVEL === 'debug') {
    console.error('Stack trace:', error.stack);
  }
}
```

### 5. Added Warning for Plugin Name Changes
**Example:**
```
$ elizaos create my-plugin --type plugin

warn: changing "my-plugin" to "plugin-my" to conform to plugin naming conventions

◇  Create plugin "plugin-my" in .?
```

- `elizaos create my-plugin --type plugin` → Shows naming warning
- `elizaos create existing-dir --yes` → Clean error message
- `elizaos create --yes` → Clean missing name error

31:T5e6,## Summary
This PR addresses the critical and important issues identified in the EventTarget migration PR review.

## Changes Made

### Critical Issues Fixed
- ✅ **Type safety in SimpleMigrationAgent**: Handler parameter already correctly typed as `(data?: unknown) => void`
- ✅ **Classes documentation**: Added documentation explaining why classes are used instead of functional patterns (EventTarget is a native API requiring class inheritance)

### Important Issues Fixed
- ✅ **Null safety in InternalMessageBus**: Added proper null checks using optional chaining in the `off()` method
- ✅ **EventEmitter behavior documentation**: Documented the intentional design choice to prevent duplicate handler registration

## Technical Details

1. **EventTarget Requirement**: Both implementations use classes because EventTarget is a native browser/Bun API that requires class inheritance. This is an architectural decision to leverage Bun's native capabilities instead of Node.js EventEmitter.

2. **Duplicate Prevention**: Unlike standard EventEmitter, these implementations prevent duplicate handler registration to avoid memory leaks and unintended multiple executions.

3. **Null Safety**: Updated the cleanup logic in `InternalMessageBus.off()` to use optional chaining for safer null handling.

## Testing
- All tests pass successfully
- No breaking changes introduced
- Maintains backward compatibility

## Related Issues
Addresses review feedback from the EventEmitter → EventTarget migration PR32:T4dd,## Summary
